   247	### 3.6 Selected row treatment (over Phase 4's 8-column table)
   248	
   249	The list-row anatomy stays Phase 4 in M1a. The only visual additions
   250	are:
   251	
   252	| Variant | Background | Other |
   253	|---------|------------|-------|
   254	| Default | transparent (inherits surface) | (Phase 4 hairline border-bottom) |
   255	| Hover | `color-mix(in srgb, var(--color-text) 4%, transparent)` | cursor: pointer |
   256	| Selected (`aria-current="true"`) | `color-mix(in srgb, var(--color-accent) 8%, transparent)` | inset 2 px sienna left edge via `box-shadow: inset 2px 0 0 var(--color-accent)` |
   257	| Selected + hover | `color-mix(in srgb, var(--color-accent) 12%, transparent)` | (selected wins; hover deepens) |
   258	| Deep-link pulse (`data-deep-link="true"`) | keyframe peak `color-mix(in srgb, var(--color-accent) 22%, transparent)` → settles to selected tint | one-shot 600 ms ease-out, URL-driven mount only |
   259	
   260	The 2 px left edge uses `box-shadow: inset 2px 0 0 var(--color-accent)`
   261	(NOT a `border-left`) so adding the inset does not change row width or
   262	shift the first cell — the row layout is byte-equivalent to Phase 4
   263	when not selected. The recipe is friendly to M2's oklch redefinition:
   264	the recipe consumes `var(--color-accent)`, not a hex.
   265	
   266	```css
   267	tr[aria-current="true"] {
   268	  background-color: color-mix(in srgb, var(--color-accent) 8%, transparent);
   269	  box-shadow: inset 2px 0 0 var(--color-accent);
   270	}
   271	tr[aria-current="true"]:hover {
   272	  background-color: color-mix(in srgb, var(--color-accent) 12%, transparent);
   273	}
   274	tr[data-deep-link="true"] {
   275	  animation: deep-link-pulse var(--motion-pulse-m1, 600ms) var(--ease-out-m1, ease-out) 1;
   276	}
   277	@keyframes deep-link-pulse {
   278	  0%   { background-color: color-mix(in srgb, var(--color-accent) 22%, transparent); }
   279	  100% { background-color: color-mix(in srgb, var(--color-accent)  8%, transparent); }
   280	}
   281	```
   282	
   283	(The fallback values `--motion-pulse-m1` / `--ease-out-m1` are
   284	**purely defensive** — if the developer leaves them undefined, CSS
   285	falls through to `600ms` and `ease-out`. M2 introduces real
   286	`--motion-pulse` / `--ease-out` tokens; until then we hard-code per
   287	spec §Structural literals line 934. The `M1` suffix in the fallback
   288	name makes the intent obvious and is a CSS custom-property naming
   289	courtesy, not a token.)
   290	
   291	## 4. States & variants
   292	
   293	### 4.1 Right-pane state matrix
   294	
   295	| `data-state` | When | Copy + visual treatment | Buttons |
   296	|--------------|------|-------------------------|---------|
   297	| `empty` | no `selectedRowKey`; not deep-linked | Two-paragraph preface (verbatim spec lines 591–593): <br/>**Para 1**: `Select a session from the list to view its content.` <br/>**Para 2**: `The session view shows the full Transcript chronologically, a Skim outline (one block per user message), the Raw NDJSON for verification, and the session's Metadata.` <br/>**Plus**: small text-only mark (a lone `·` middle-dot or `§` glyph at `var(--text-xl)` weight 200 in `var(--color-text-muted)`) centered above the prose. Decision: text-only over SVG illustration — see §6.1. | none |
   298	| `loading` | `selectedRowKey` set AND any of source/stored/scan-errors GETs in flight AND the row hasn't merged yet | Single short line: `Reading session…` in `var(--color-text-muted)` `var(--text-sm)`. No spinner. Rationale: the editorial mood prefers quiet over busy — and the 5 MB cap (M3) bounds the wait. | none |
   299	| `ready-placeholder` | row IS selected and merged in; M1a has no tabs yet | Single short line: `Session view coming in Milestone 2.` in `var(--color-text-muted)` `var(--text-sm)`. Optional secondary line (deferred — see §6.2): "Tabs (Transcript, Skim, Raw, Metadata) land in M2." | none in M1a (M1b adds the vestigial "Open detail" button) |
   300	| `session_not_found` | URL `?session=<rowKey>` AND all three GETs settled AND no row matches | Two-line message: <br/>**Heading**: `Session not found in current view` (`var(--text-lg)`, `var(--color-text)`) <br/>**Hint**: `The session referenced by the URL was not in the merged set after the latest scan.` (`var(--text-sm)`, `var(--color-text-muted)`) <br/>Plus two buttons (see right column). | `Clear selection` (quiet button — text + 1 px hairline border, no fill) → calls `selectRow(null)`, removes the URL `?session=` param. <br/>`Try Rescan` (quiet button) → calls `refetchAll()` (App.tsx). |
   301	
   302	### 4.2 Layout variants
   303	
   304	| Layout | `narrowMode` | List visible? | Session visible? | Hairline gutter? | Back-to-list visible? |
   305	|--------|--------------|---------------|-------------------|-------------------|------------------------|
   306	| Wide (≥ 900 px) | n/a (ignored) | yes | yes | yes | no |
   307	| Narrow + list | `"list"` | yes (full-width) | no | no (only one pane) | no |
   308	| Narrow + session | `"session"` | no | yes (full-width) | no | yes |
   309	| Narrow + session + no `selectedRowKey` | should not occur | the "Back to list" semantics ensure narrowMode falls back to "list" if selection clears | — | — | — |
   310	
   311	**Unreachable-state recovery**: if a future code path lands the user
   312	in `narrowMode === "session"` with `selectedRowKey === null`, the
   313	right pane renders the `empty` state and the developer is expected to
   314	synchronously revert to `narrowMode = "list"`. This is documented in
   315	the implementation acceptance checklist (§8 item 18).
   316	
   317	### 4.3 List-row variants (over Phase-4 8-column table)
   318	
   319	| Variant | DOM / attr | CSS recipe |
   320	|---------|-----------|------------|
   321	| Default | `<tr>` | (Phase 4 baseline) |
   322	| Hover | `<tr>:hover` | `background-color: color-mix(in srgb, var(--color-text) 4%, transparent)` |
   323	| Selected | `<tr aria-current="true">` | tint + inset 2 px sienna left edge |
   324	| Selected + hover | `<tr aria-current="true">:hover` | deeper tint |
   325	| Deep-link pulse | `<tr data-deep-link="true">` (one-shot, URL-driven mount only) | 600 ms keyframe; cleared by `onAnimationEnd` OR 2 s safety timer |
   326	
   327	## 5. Motion & interaction
   328	
   329	### 5.1 Deep-link pulse (signature-detail #3)
   330	
   331	Trigger: **URL-driven mount only.** When the app mounts and
   332	`URLSearchParams(window.location.search).get("session")` is non-null,
   333	`App.tsx` writes a `pendingDeepLinkPulseRowKey` alongside
   334	`selectedRowKey`. The matched row reads that and applies
   335	`data-deep-link="true"` for one paint cycle.
   336	
   337	Animation:
   338	- **Duration**: 600 ms (spec §Motion table; structural literal §938).
   339	- **Easing**: `ease-out` (cubic-bezier(0.0, 0.0, 0.2, 1)).
   340	- **Property animated**: `background-color` only (composite-cheap; on
   341	  the spec's allow-list at line 1100).
   342	- **Keyframe**: peak `color-mix(in srgb, var(--color-accent) 22%, transparent)` →
   343	  settles to the selected-row tint
   344	  `color-mix(in srgb, var(--color-accent) 8%, transparent)`.
   345	- **One-shot**: `animation-iteration-count: 1`.
   346	
   347	Cleanup (whichever fires first):
   348	- The row's `onAnimationEnd` handler clears `pendingDeepLinkPulseRowKey`.
   349	- A 2-second `setTimeout` from initial mount clears it unconditionally
   350	  (safety net: `session_not_found` may mean the row never renders).
   351	
   352	**Click-driven selection NEVER fires the pulse.** This is the
   353	defining distinction in spec line 585. The user already knows which
   354	row they clicked; the pulse only orients a returning URL-paste user.
   355	
   356	### 5.2 Reduced-motion zero-out
   357	
   358	Single global rule in `global.css`. Mirrors spec §Motion lines 102–110:
   359	
   360	```css
   361	@media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce) {
   362	  *, *::before, *::after {
   363	    animation-duration: 0.01ms !important;
   364	    animation-iteration-count: 1 !important;
   365	    transition-duration: 0.01ms !important;
   366	    scroll-behavior: auto !important;
   367	  }
   368	}
   369	```
   370	
   371	Effect on M1a:
   372	- The 600 ms deep-link pulse collapses to 0.01 ms (effectively
   373	  invisible — the row renders directly in its selected-tint state).
   374	- The selected-row tint itself is **NOT animated** in M1a (the spec
   375	  introduces the `--motion-base` transition timing in M2). So
   376	  reduced-motion has nothing to suppress on selection state changes
   377	  in M1a — the tint just snaps in.
   378	
   379	Verification: with reduced-motion on, every transition completes
   380	within one paint frame.
   381	
   382	### 5.3 Interaction model: Esc vs "← Back to list" (Resolved Decision #17)
   383	
   384	| Gesture | Effect | Scope |
   385	|---------|--------|-------|
   386	| Click on row | `setSelectedRowKey(rowKey)` + `replaceState(buildUrl(rowKey))` + (narrow only) `setNarrowMode("session")` | always |
   387	| `← Back to list` button (narrow only) | `setNarrowMode("list")` ONLY | preserves `selectedRowKey`, URL, scroll, tab state |
   388	| `Esc` key | `setSelectedRowKey(null)` + `replaceState(buildUrl(null))` + `setNarrowMode("list")` | **scoped**: ignored when focus is in `<input>`, `<textarea>`, `[contenteditable]`, `role="combobox"`. Active when focus is in the session pane OR on a selected row. |
   389	| `popstate` (Back/Forward) | re-read URL, sync `selectedRowKey`. Does NOT call `replaceState`. | always |
   390	
   391	**Why Back-to-list is a quiet text link, not a chunky button**:
   392	the editorial mood. Chunky buttons read like primary actions; the
   393	right-pane primary actions live in the M2 tab strip. The
   394	"Back to list" gesture is meta-navigation, not a destination.
   395	
   396	**Why Esc is scoped**: the Phase-4 `SessionFilters` search input
   397	already consumes Esc to clear its own value. Letting the global Esc
   398	handler swallow that gesture would regress Phase-4 filter UX. The
   399	scope rule (ignored in editable controls) preserves the existing
   400	behavior.
   401	
   402	### 5.4 Loading-while-fetching transition
   403	
   404	When a deep-linked row hasn't merged in yet (any of the three GETs in
   405	flight + `?session=` is set), the right pane is in `loading` state.
   406	The transition from `loading` → `ready-placeholder` (or →
   407	`session_not_found`) is **not animated** in M1a — the page-turn fade
   408	(signature-detail #2) is an M2/M4 deliverable. M1a just snaps state.
   409	This is editorial-mood-aligned and code-cheap.
   410	
   411	## 6. Accessibility
   412	
   413	### 6.1 Landmarks
   414	
   415	- **List pane**: `<aside aria-label="Sessions list">` — complementary
   416	  landmark. Assistive tech announces it as a complementary region.
   417	- **Session pane**: `<article>` — the spec-mandated wrapper (spec
   418	  line 1121). I deliberately did **not** add `role="main"` because
   419	  the surrounding `<main>` is already the document main landmark
   420	  and adding `role="main"` to a child would create competing main

    29	
    30	## 2. Design intent
    31	
    32	### Why these visible surfaces exist now
    33	
    34	The Phase 4 inspection page is a single full-width `<main>` that
    35	overlays a modal `<Drawer>` for session detail. Phase 5 dissolves the
    36	modal and runs the user along a **single document, two views**: the
    37	list on the left, the session on the right. M1a builds the bones for
    38	that — the grid, the URL hook, the right pane stub — without yet
    39	moving any list chrome (M1b) or wiring real session content (M2 → M5).
    40	
    41	### Archive-room anchoring at M1a
    42	
    43	Per Resolved Decision #10, Phase 5 commits to the Archive-room
    44	aesthetic: warm-paper light / deep-ink dark, sienna single accent,
    45	hairlines over shadows, sharp panel corners, restrained motion.
    46	M1a's surface-defining gesture is **the hairline gutter** — a single
    47	1 px line at `var(--color-border)` that says "one document, two
    48	views" without rails, shadows, or chrome. Pair that with the
    49	**deep-link pulse** — a 600 ms warm-amber breath when the user
    50	arrives via URL — and M1a already telegraphs the editorial tone the
    51	typography/oklch token rollout in M2 will fully voice.
    52	
    53	The placeholder right-pane is intentionally quiet. It is not a
    54	spinner-driven "loading dashboard" surface; it is a brief
    55	two-paragraph preface that reads like the inside-cover note of a
    56	printed reference book. The shape of that copy is fixed by spec
    57	lines 591–593.
    58	
    59	### What M1a sets up for M2
    60	
    61	- **Right-pane container** is wrapped in `<article>` and given the
    62	  CSS hooks (`.session-pane`, `[data-state]`) that M2's Tabs
    63	  primitive will mount inside.
    64	- **Selected-row tint** uses the spec-mandated `color-mix` recipe
    65	  against `var(--color-accent)`. M2 redefines `--color-accent` in
    66	  oklch; the recipe is unchanged. The pulse keyframe also references
    67	  `--color-accent` so M2 retints automatically.
    68	- **`<aside>` and `<article>` landmarks** mean the M2 Tabs primitive
    69	  does not need to introduce additional `role="region"` — both panes
    70	  are already complementary / main-document landmarks.
    71	- **Reduced-motion zero-out rule** is the global rule M2's Tabs slide
    72	  + M4's transcript fade + M5's skim-block stagger all rely on. M1a
    73	  drops it once into `global.css` so subsequent chunks reuse it.
    74	
    75	## 3. Component anatomy
    76	
    77	### 3.1 DOM tree (wide viewport, row selected, no deep-link arrival)
    78	
    79	```
    80	<body>
    81	  <main class="split-pane" data-narrow-mode="list">         <!-- root grid, see §3.2 -->
    82	    <aside class="list-pane" aria-label="Sessions list">    <!-- left landmark -->
    83	      <!-- Phase 4 chrome stays exactly as today during M1a:
    84	           SessionFilters, SessionsTable (8 columns), Pagination,
    85	           ActionBar — all still rendered by SessionsView.tsx
    86	           in their existing positions. M1b will relocate
    87	           Pagination + ActionBar to a sticky list-pane footer
    88	           and compress the table to 4 columns.
    89	
    90	           The only M1a change to the list pane DOM is the
    91	           wrapping <aside> landmark + the border-right gutter
    92	           drawn by .list-pane CSS, plus the new
    93	           aria-current="true" attribute on the selected row
    94	           (added by SessionsTable consumers when they receive
    95	           selectedRowKey). -->
    96	      …Phase 4 list children…
    97	    </aside>
    98	
    99	    <article class="session-pane" data-state="ready-placeholder">
   100	      <!-- Right pane. <article> is the spec-mandated
   101	           accessibility landmark (spec line 1121). The
   102	           data-state attribute drives both ARIA live-region
   103	           politeness and the visible state machine:
   104	           "empty" | "loading" | "ready-placeholder" |
   105	           "session_not_found". -->
   106	      <header class="session-pane__header">
   107	        <p class="session-pane__hint">Session view coming in Milestone 2.</p>
   108	      </header>
   109	    </article>
   110	  </main>
   111	
   112	  <!-- Toast queue stays a sibling of <main> (Phase 4 pattern) -->
   113	</body>
   114	```
   115	
   116	### 3.2 `<main class="split-pane">` recipe
   117	
   118	| Property | Wide (≥ 900 px) | Narrow (< 900 px) |
   119	|----------|-----------------|-------------------|
   120	| `display` | `grid` | `grid` |
   121	| `grid-template-columns` | `minmax(300px, 380px) 1fr` | `1fr` |
   122	| `gap` | `0` | `0` |
   123	| `max-width` | `1400px` (Phase-4 baseline) | `1400px` |
   124	| `margin` | `0 auto` | `0 auto` |
   125	| `padding` | `0` | `0` |
   126	
   127	(The `body` keeps its Phase-4 `padding: var(--space-6)` so the page
   128	gutters do not collapse to the viewport edge — that one carries
   129	over verbatim. The M1a `<main>` recipe, by contrast, intentionally
   130	**replaces** Phase 4's `<main>` rules entirely: Phase 4's
   131	`padding: 0 var(--space-4)`, `margin: var(--space-6) auto var(--space-8)`,
   132	and `gap: var(--space-6)` are all dropped, because the spec at
   133	§Inspection Surface Layout line 497 prescribes a fresh recipe
   134	(`max-width: 1400px; margin: 0 auto; gap: 0; padding: 0`). So the
   135	relationship is "preserve body gutter, replace `<main>` recipe in
   136	full" — not "exactly like Phase 4". `<main>` itself contributes no
   137	padding; its children's panes own their internal padding.)
   138	
   139	Activation:
   140	
   141	```css
   142	.split-pane { grid-template-columns: 1fr; }
   143	@media (min-width: 900px) {
   144	  .split-pane { grid-template-columns: minmax(300px, 380px) 1fr; }
   145	}
   146	```
   147	
   148	(`min-width: 900px` matches the spec acceptance criterion at
   149	exactly 900 px → split-pane.)
   150	
   151	### 3.3 List pane (`<aside class="list-pane">`)
   152	
   153	| Property | Wide | Narrow + `narrowMode="list"` | Narrow + `narrowMode="session"` |
   154	|----------|------|------------------------------|----------------------------------|
   155	| Visibility | always rendered | full width | `display: none` |
   156	| `border-right` | `1px solid var(--color-border)` | none | none |
   157	| `min-inline-size` | `300px` | `100%` | n/a |
   158	| `max-inline-size` | `380px` | `100%` | n/a |
   159	| Internal layout | unchanged from Phase 4 | unchanged | unchanged |
   160	
   161	The `border-right` is the **hairline gutter** (signature-detail #4).
   162	It only renders on wide viewports because the narrow-mode CSS hides
   163	the list pane when `narrowMode === "session"` and there is no second
   164	pane to gutter against when `narrowMode === "list"`.
   165	
   166	```css
   167	.list-pane {
   168	  /* No border on narrow viewports — gutter only exists between two
   169	     visible panes. */
   170	}
   171	@media (min-width: 900px) {
   172	  .list-pane {
   173	    border-right: 1px solid var(--color-border);
   174	  }
   175	}
   176	@media (max-width: 899.98px) {
   177	  .split-pane[data-narrow-mode="session"] .list-pane { display: none; }
   178	}
   179	```
   180	
   181	(The `899.98px` literal is spec-mandated — line 516 — to avoid the
   182	inclusive-vs-exclusive ambiguity at the boundary.)
   183	
   184	### 3.4 Session pane (`<article class="session-pane">`)
   185	
   186	| Property | Value |
   187	|----------|-------|
   188	| Element | `<article>` (spec line 1121) |
   189	| Wrapping role | landmark `article`; assistive tech announces it as a self-contained section |
   190	| `aria-busy` | `"true"` while `data-state === "loading"`; otherwise `"false"` |
   191	| `aria-live` | `"polite"` (so right-pane state changes are announced once) |
   192	| `data-state` | `"empty" \| "loading" \| "ready-placeholder" \| "session_not_found"` — drives both styling and screen-reader scope |
   193	| Padding | `var(--space-8) var(--space-6)` (≈ 32 px / 24 px). Generous, editorial. |
   194	| Background | `var(--color-bg)` (Phase-4 baseline; M2 swaps to warm-paper / deep-ink) |
   195	| Min-height | none (hugs content) on M1a; M2 sets `min-block-size: 60vh` once tabs ship |
   196	
   197	| Narrow visibility | rule |
   198	|-------------------|------|
   199	| `narrowMode="list"` | `display: none` |
   200	| `narrowMode="session"` | `display: block` (full width) |
   201	
   202	```css
   203	.session-pane {
   204	  padding: var(--space-8) var(--space-6);
   205	  /* state-driven content styled per state; see §4 */
   206	}
   207	@media (max-width: 899.98px) {
   208	  .split-pane[data-narrow-mode="list"] .session-pane { display: none; }
   209	}
   210	```
   211	
   212	### 3.5 Back-to-list affordance (narrow viewport only)
   213	
   214	| Property | Value |
   215	|----------|-------|
   216	| Element | `<button type="button" class="back-to-list">` |
   217	| Visible | only when `narrowMode === "session"` AND viewport < 900 px |
   218	| Position | first child of `.session-pane`, before `.session-pane__header` |
   219	| Label | `← Back to list` (literal arrow glyph + space + text) |
   220	| Style | quiet text-link (no border, no fill, just text color + underline on hover/focus). Distinguished visually from primary actions. |

// Playwright config for the inspection-surface browser e2e.
//
// Chromium-only — no Firefox/WebKit, no visual-regression snapshots, no
// CI wiring. Tests run serially (`workers: 1`) because the harness binds
// the Rust backend to port 4000, matching the Vite dev proxy in
// `vite.config.ts`; parallel workers would collide.
//
// Intentional omission: the backend is NOT declared as a `webServer`
// here. The spec's `test.beforeAll` starts it via
// `e2e/harness/backend.ts` so it can seed fixtures into the same temp
// dir the backend was pointed at; Playwright's webServer hook can't
// reach back into the spec to share that path. Vite is the only

 succeeded in 0ms:
// Backend spawn harness for Playwright e2e.
//
// Launches `cargo run -p distill-portal-backend` as a child process and
// returns a handle whose `.stop()` terminates it and cleans up the temp
// dir. Bound to 127.0.0.1:4000 because the Vite dev server proxies
// `/api/v1/**` and `/health` to that address (see
// `apps/frontend/vite.config.ts`); pointing the browser at a dynamic
// port would require rewriting the proxy on every run. Tests run
// serially (`workers: 1` in playwright.config.ts) so port 4000 is safe.
//
// Subprocess creation MUST use `Bun.spawn` — Bun-first rule. `node:fs`
// and `node:path` are used only for their sync/async filesystem
// helpers (creating a temp dir, writing a fixture).
import { mkdtemp, mkdir, writeFile, rm } from "node:fs/promises";
import { tmpdir } from "node:os";
import { dirname, join } from "node:path";
import { fileURLToPath } from "node:url";

// `import.meta.dir` is Bun-only; Playwright invokes this module under
// Node's ESM loader, so we derive the directory from the URL instead.
const __dirname = dirname(fileURLToPath(import.meta.url));

export type BackendHandle = {
  /** HTTP base URL the backend listens on, including the scheme. */
  baseUrl: string;
  /** Filesystem path the backend treats as the Claude source root. */
  claudeRoot: string;
  /** Root temp dir owning `claudeRoot`, `codexRoot`, and the backend's data dir. */
  tempDir: string;
  /** Terminate the child process and remove the temp dir. Safe to call twice. */
  stop: () => Promise<void>;
};

export type StartBackendOptions = {
  /** Seed a Claude-code jsonl fixture at `<claudeRoot>/<claudeProject>/<claudeSessionId>.jsonl`. */
  seed?: {
    /** Project-directory name under the Claude root (matches Claude Code on-disk layout). */
    claudeProject: string;
    claudeSessionId: string;
    /** Raw NDJSON bytes to write. */
    jsonl: string | Uint8Array;
  };
  /**
   * Port the backend binds to. Hard-coded to 4000 because the Vite dev
   * proxy targets `http://127.0.0.1:4000`; overridable in case that
   * assumption ever changes.
   */
  port?: number;
  /** Max millis to wait for `/health` to return 200. Cold `cargo run` can be slow. */
  readyTimeoutMs?: number;
  /** Poll-interval override so freshly-seeded sessions appear quickly. */
  pollIntervalSecs?: number;
};

/**
 * Start the Rust backend in a temp-dir sandbox and wait until
 * `GET /health` returns 200. Resolves to a `BackendHandle` whose
 * `.stop()` tears everything down.
 */
export async function startBackend(
  options: StartBackendOptions = {},
): Promise<BackendHandle> {
  const port = options.port ?? 4000;
  const readyTimeoutMs = options.readyTimeoutMs ?? 120_000;
  const pollIntervalSecs = options.pollIntervalSecs ?? 1;

  const tempDir = await mkdtemp(join(tmpdir(), "distill-portal-e2e-"));
  const dataDir = join(tempDir, "data");
  const claudeRoot = join(tempDir, "claude", "projects");
  const codexRoot = join(tempDir, "codex", "sessions");
  await mkdir(dataDir, { recursive: true });
  await mkdir(claudeRoot, { recursive: true });
  await mkdir(codexRoot, { recursive: true });

  if (options.seed) {
    const projectDir = join(claudeRoot, options.seed.claudeProject);
    await mkdir(projectDir, { recursive: true });
    const sessionPath = join(
      projectDir,
      `${options.seed.claudeSessionId}.jsonl`,
    );
    await writeFile(sessionPath, options.seed.jsonl);
  }

  const baseUrl = `http://127.0.0.1:${port}`;

  // Bun.spawn: Bun-first rule. Never `child_process.spawn` in this repo.
  // `cargo run` inherits our env. We pin every variable the backend reads
  // so `BackendConfig::load()` resolves them deterministically — see
  // `components/configuration/src/lib.rs`.
  const workspaceRoot = join(__dirname, "..", "..", "..", "..");
  const proc = Bun.spawn({
    cmd: ["cargo", "run", "-p", "distill-portal-backend"],
    cwd: workspaceRoot,
    env: {
      ...process.env,
      DISTILL_PORTAL_BACKEND_BIND: `127.0.0.1:${port}`,
      DISTILL_PORTAL_DATA_DIR: dataDir,
      DISTILL_PORTAL_CLAUDE_ROOTS: claudeRoot,
      // Point Codex at an empty dir so the scanner doesn't touch the
      // developer's actual ~/.codex/sessions directory.
      DISTILL_PORTAL_CODEX_ROOTS: codexRoot,
      DISTILL_PORTAL_POLL_INTERVAL_SECS: String(pollIntervalSecs),
      // Keep log output concise so Playwright's stdout isn't flooded.
      RUST_LOG: process.env.RUST_LOG ?? "error",
    },
    stdout: "inherit",
    stderr: "inherit",
  });

  let stopped = false;
  const stop = async (): Promise<void> => {
    if (stopped) return;
    stopped = true;
    try {
      proc.kill("SIGTERM");
      // Give the child 10s to exit cleanly before abandoning it.
      const exited = await Promise.race([
        proc.exited,
        new Promise<"timeout">((resolve) =>
          setTimeout(() => resolve("timeout"), 10_000),
        ),
      ]);
      if (exited === "timeout") {
        proc.kill("SIGKILL");
        await proc.exited;
      }
    } catch {
      // Already exited.
    }
    try {
      await rm(tempDir, { recursive: true, force: true });
    } catch {
      // Best-effort; leaving junk in /tmp is not test-blocking.
    }
  };

  try {
    await waitForHealth(baseUrl, readyTimeoutMs, proc);
  } catch (error) {
    await stop();
    throw error;
  }

  return { baseUrl, claudeRoot, tempDir, stop };
}

async function waitForHealth(
  baseUrl: string,
  timeoutMs: number,
  proc: { exited: Promise<number> },
): Promise<void> {
  const start = Date.now();
  const healthUrl = `${baseUrl}/health`;
  let lastError: unknown = null;

  let childExited = false;
  // Fail fast if the child dies during startup (e.g. port already bound).
  const exitWatcher = proc.exited.then((code) => {
    childExited = true;
    lastError = new Error(`backend exited with code ${code} before /health became ready`);
  });
  void exitWatcher;

  while (Date.now() - start < timeoutMs) {
    if (childExited) {
      throw lastError ?? new Error("backend exited during startup");
    }
    try {
      const response = await fetch(healthUrl);
      if (response.ok) {
        // Drain the body so the connection releases cleanly.
        await response.arrayBuffer();
        return;
      }
      lastError = new Error(`/health returned ${response.status}`);
    } catch (error) {
      lastError = error;
    }
    await new Promise((resolve) => setTimeout(resolve, 250));
  }
  throw new Error(
    `backend did not become ready within ${timeoutMs}ms: ${String(lastError)}`,
  );
}

**Verdict**: needs changes

**Findings**:
- blocking — [SessionView.css](/home/huwei/ai_codings/distill-portal/apps/frontend/src/features/sessions/SessionView.css:136): `.back-to-list` is always `display: none`, and there is no override. The narrow “← Back to list” affordance renders in React but is invisible/inaccessible, breaking the M1a narrow-viewport requirement.
- blocking — [App.tsx](/home/huwei/ai_codings/distill-portal/apps/frontend/src/App.tsx:701): Esc clears selection for every non-editable target. Spec line 567 scopes it to the session pane or a selected row. This can clear selection from unrelated controls.
- blocking — [App.tsx](/home/huwei/ai_codings/distill-portal/apps/frontend/src/App.tsx:685): contenteditable filtering only matches `[contenteditable="true"]`, not `[contenteditable]` as required. Empty or `plaintext-only` contenteditable elements would incorrectly clear selection on Esc.
- blocking — [inspection.spec.ts](/home/huwei/ai_codings/distill-portal/apps/frontend/e2e/inspection.spec.ts:469): e2e step 12 does not verify the deep-link pulse; it only asserts stable `aria-current`. Unit tests cover the data attribute in happy-dom, but the claimed browser evidence for the pulse/animation is unsupported.

**Missing Evidence**:
- Browser or component evidence that the narrow Back-to-list button is visible/clickable under `<900px` and preserves selection + URL.
- Tests for full Esc scope: session pane clears, selected row clears, unrelated non-editable focus does not clear, and input/textarea/contenteditable/combobox are ignored.
- Browser-level deep-link pulse assertion, or the claim should be narrowed to unit coverage.

**Required Changes**:
- Make `.back-to-list` visible when it is rendered, or add the intended `@media (max-width: 899.98px) main.split-pane[data-narrow-mode="session"] .back-to-list { display: inline-block; }`.
- Tighten the Esc handler to the spec scope and match `[contenteditable]` broadly.
- Add tests for the narrow Back-to-list visibility/action and full Esc scope.
- Strengthen or correct the e2e deep-link pulse evidence.

**Notes**:
- Protected paths stayed untouched. `package.json`/lock were unchanged. No new router/runtime dependency evidence found.
- Hex count confirmed `24`; token count confirmed `44`.
- `bun run test` passed: 253 tests / 976 expects. `bun run build` passed. My e2e rerun timed out waiting for the Vite webServer in this sandbox, so I did not independently reproduce the coordinator’s e2e green run.
